03
L1factmodelContext

03 · Dynamic context enters the next request through a projection

SOURCE FACT

preStep renders prompt sections through RuntimeContextProjection.project and appends the returned UserMessage to claimed messages.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Context is not permanently baked into the system prompt; it is generated from session state as a traceable message.

IMPLICATION

Dynamic environment context needs a budget, source, and version rather than hidden injection.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 225–232
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 233–242
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/core/agent-loop/src/agent.ts:225–242PINNED ↗
  225    private async preStep(target: InboxTarget, position: { turn: number; step: number }): Promise<PreparedStep> {
  226      /* v8 ignore next -- private callers establish the running phase before proposing a step */
  227      if (this.phase.kind !== 'running') throw new Error(`agent "${this.id}": pre-step outside running phase`)
  228      const signal = this.phase.abort.signal
  229      const claimed = this.inbox.claim(target, position.turn)
  230      const assembly = await this.loopCtx.systemPrompt.assemble(assembleContextFor(this, signal))
  231      signal.throwIfAborted()
  232      const sections = renderContextSections(assembly)
  233      const context = this.runtimeContext.project(joinContextSections(sections), sections)
  234      const decision = await this.dispatch.waterfall(
  235        'agent/pre-step', { messages: claimed, ...position, signal },
  236        (): Promise<PreStepDecision> => Promise.resolve<PreStepDecision>({
  237          kind: 'enter',
  238          messages: context === undefined ? claimed : [...claimed, context],
  239        }),
  240      )
  241      signal.throwIfAborted()
  242      return decision.kind === 'reject' ? decision : { ...decision, assembly }
  243    }
  244
  245    /** Open one turn before claiming its first proposed step. */
  246    private async turn(): Promise<boolean> {
  247      if (this.phase.kind !== 'running') {
  248        this.throwError(new Error(`agent "${this.id}": turn without driver reservation`))
  249      }
  250      const phase = this.phase
  251      const { signal } = phase.abort
  252      signal.throwIfAborted()
  253      const turn = phase.turn + 1
  254      try {
  255        this.session.append('turn/start', { turn })
  256      } catch (error: unknown) {
  257        this.throwError(error)
  258      }
  259      phase.turn = turn
  260      let turnEnds: TurnEndReason | null = null
  261      let target: InboxTarget = 'next-turn'
  262      try {
  263        while (true) {
  264          signal.throwIfAborted()
  265          const step = phase.step + 1
  266          const decision = await this.preStep(target, { turn, step })
  267          if (decision.kind === 'reject') {
  268            turnEnds = { kind: 'blocked' }
  269            return false
  270          }
  271          if (turnEnds && decision.messages.length === 0) break
  272          // A removed waking message or an enter decision rewritten to empty
  273          // still owns the initial turn boundary, but it spends no model call.
  274          if (phase.step === 0 && decision.messages.length === 0) {
  275            turnEnds = { kind: 'completed' }
  276            return false
  277          }
  278          signal.throwIfAborted()
  279          this.session.append('step/start', { turn, step })
  280          phase.step = step
  281          try {
  282            for (const message of decision.messages) {
  283              this.session.append('user/message', message, { surfaceOp: 'append' })
  284            }
  285            // max-tokens is sticky: once any step hits the ceiling, later steps
  286            // that complete normally must not downgrade the turn outcome.
  287            const stepEnd = await this.step(decision.assembly)
  288            // max-tokens stays sticky: a later completed step must not
  289            // downgrade the turn outcome.
  290            if (turnEnds === null || turnEnds.kind !== 'max-tokens') turnEnds = stepEnd
  291          } finally {
  292            this.session.append('step/end', { turn, step })
  293          }
  294          signal.throwIfAborted()
  295          if (turnEnds && this.inbox.nextStep.length === 0) {
  296            await this.dispatch.serial('agent/turn-stopping', { turn, signal })
  297            signal.throwIfAborted()
  298          }
  299          if (turnEnds && this.inbox.nextStep.length === 0) break
  300          target = 'next-step'
  301        }
  302      } catch (error: unknown) {
  303        if (signal.aborted) {
  304          turnEnds = { kind: 'aborted', reason: signal.reason as AgentCancelCause }
  305          throw error
  306        }
  307        // Every failure is structured: an `LlmError` keeps its facts, anything
  308        // else flattens to `errorChain` text under the `UNKNOWN` code.
  309        turnEnds = {
  310          kind: 'error',
  311          error: error instanceof LlmError
  312            ? error.failure
  313            : { message: errorChain(error), code: 'UNKNOWN' },
Expand 28 line annotations

04
L4factmodelContext

04 · Model-visible input must be reconstructable from the session log

SOURCE FACT

architecture.md states model-visible ⟺ logged: anything reaching a model request must have an event source and be projected by deriveMessages.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

If context exists only in memory, a restart makes the model forget it; it is not a durable input.

IMPLICATION

When adding context, add the event first and then the prompt projection.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 95–102
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 103–112
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

docs/architecture.md:95–112PINNED ↗
   95
   96  **Model-visible means logged.** Anything that reaches a model request must be reconstructable from the log, and a runtime invariant asserts it. This is why a new model-visible input requires a new session event: extend `SessionEventMap` and render from the log.
   97
   98  ## Capability seams
   99
  100  A **seam** is a swappable capability with three roles: a **Service Definition** declaring the interface, a **Service Provider** implementing it, and a **Consumer** using it, commonly a model-facing tool. A package may combine roles, but one role alone is not a seam; adding a capability means designing all three ([capability graph](capability-seams.md)).
  101
  102  Seams are why one provider swap changes the whole product. Filesystem and subprocess providers share one execution world, so pointing them at a remote sandbox moves Bash, PTY, and LSP with them, with no provider forks. [Subagent providers](subsystems/subagent.md) vary just as widely behind one interface, from a fresh child agent to a delegated turn in another product.
  103
  104  ## Where new behavior goes
  105
  106  New behavior attaches to a documented extension point. Changing the loop itself updates this map.
  107
  108  | Goal | Mechanism |
  109  |---|---|
  110  | Add a model provider | register its adapter on `ctx.llm` |
  111  | Add a model-facing capability | register on `ctx.tools`; its schema joins prompt assembly |
  112  | Give one session a different capability set | compose an agent preset; a service row there needs an `isolate` realm |
  113  | Add shell execution | register a `ctx.shell` backend; the local one spawns through `ctx.subprocess` |
  114  | Add persistent terminal execution | register a `ctx.terminals` backend plus `dsh-tool-terminal` |
  115  | Add a human command | register on `ctx.commands`; it dispatches without a model turn |
  116  | Add background work | register on `ctx.jobs`; `job_*` tools collect or stop it |
  117  | Add filesystem access or policy | register a `ctx.fs` provider or listen to `fs/*` events |
  118  | Confine spawned processes | use a `ctx.sandbox` backend; consumers wrap argv before spawning |
  119  | Intercept a request, tool, or turn | use its `agent/*` or `tools/*` event; `agent/turn-stopping` stops a turn |
  120  | Add model-facing context | call `agent.inject()`; it lands in the next admitted request |
  121  | Add UI or editor integration | drive `ctx.agents` and render from `session/event` |
  122  | Add a Web Client Chat node | register a `ConversationNodeDefinition` + keyed renderer |
  123  | Add durable session state | extend `SessionEventMap`; render and replay from the log |
  124  | Generate session titles | register the sole `ctx.sessionTitle` provider |
  125  | Manage a same-session objective | use `ctx.goals`; continue through `agent/*` |
  126  | Fork a live session | `ctx.sessions.fork(source, boundary?, childSessionId?)` |
  127  | Scope a registration to one agent | use that agent's `agent.ctx` |
  128
  129  The [extension cookbook](cookbook/extension-cookbook.md) maps features to capabilities and indexes the step-by-step guides for [packages](cookbook/adding-a-package.md), [tools](cookbook/adding-a-tool.md), [LLM adapters](cookbook/adding-an-llm-adapter.md), and [Chat nodes](cookbook/adding-a-conversation-node.md).
  130

DIMENSION · EXECUTION

Execution & sandbox

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factexecution

01 · The sandbox provider selects a runner before wrapping argv

SOURCE FACT

sandbox-local defines bwrap→Landlock on Linux, Seatbelt on macOS, and restricted-token ACL on Windows; confine returns runner argv, enforcement, and failure rules.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

The executor receives an OS-constrained command, not the raw argv.

IMPLICATION

Sandboxing should be an execution seam shared by shell, PTY, and LSP consumers.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 150–167
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 168–186
    Branches, effects, and receipts

  150  /**
  151   * The runner chain per platform — selection is BY PLATFORM first, probes
  152   * second: a platform's chain is probed in preference order only when it has
  153   * MORE than one candidate (probing arbitrates; it does not re-validate a
  154   * choice that has no alternative). A platform with no chain fails closed at
  155   * `confine()`. Linux prefers `bwrap` (its mount profile is closest to the
  156   * mode vocabulary) over the Landlock launcher; darwin has exactly one
  157   * candidate, selected without any probe.

  160    linux: ['bwrap', 'landlock'],
  161    darwin: ['seatbelt'],
  162    // The Windows restricted-token runner (@deepseek-ai/dsh-sandbox-windows-acl):
  163    // a sole candidate, selected without a probe — its execution-time refusal
  164    // fails closed through its stderr signature (windows-acl-run:) and exit 127.
  165    win32: ['windows-acl'],

  168  /**
  169   * Enforcement completeness a rung claims when selected WITHOUT a probe (a
  170   * chain of one). `bwrap` and Seatbelt govern every promised file effect by
  171   * construction, so the claim is a profile fact; `landlock` is listed for the
  172   * table's totality but is unreachable unprobed today (the Linux chain has
  173   * two rungs, so it is only ever selected through its probe, whose report is
  174   * what distinguishes full from per-ABI-partial — and the launcher additionally
  175   * self-reports partial enforcement on stderr at every confined run).
  176   */
  177  const STATIC_ENFORCEMENT: Record<SelectedRunner['runner'], SandboxEnforcement> = {
  178    bwrap: 'full',
  179    landlock: 'full',
  180    seatbelt: 'full',
  181    // WRITE_RESTRICTED needs Everyone in both restricting lists for process
  182    // initialization. An external object that grants Everyone write access
  183    // therefore remains writable, and NTFS hard links can alias a granted
  184    // workspace file to a path outside it. The backend enforces the remaining
  185    // ACL-addressable surface but must not advertise the absolute promise.
  186    'windows-acl': 'partial',
  187  }
  188
  189  /**
  190   * A probe bound must be a positive finite number: Node treats
  191   * `spawnSync({ timeout: 0 })` as NO timeout, so an unvalidated 0 would
  192   * silently mean "unbounded" — the opposite of what the field promises.
  193   */
  194  function assertPositiveFinite(name: string, value: number): void {
  195    if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value <= 0) {
  196      throw new Error(`sandbox-local: ${name} must be a positive finite number`)
  197    }
  198  }

  201   * The denial dialect each runner's kernel speaks — the case-insensitive stderr substrings a

  205  const DENIAL_SIGNATURES = {
  206    bwrap: ['read-only file system'],
  207    landlock: ['permission denied'],
  208    seatbelt: ['operation not permitted'],

  210    // node EACCES: "permission denied".

  216  const WINDOWS_ACL_RUNNER_FAILURE_EXIT = 127

  218  /**
  219   * Runner-owned fatal diagnostics. Landlock has a versioned exit-125 plus
  220   * fatal-line launcher-failure contract. Bubblewrap's current fatal paths exit
  221   * 1 but its public contract does not reserve that status, while sandbox-exec
  222   * publishes no launcher-failure status; those backends remain signature-only.

  225   * so a confined command that merely PRINTS the signature (or a runner

  240  } as const satisfies Record<SelectedRunner['runner'], readonly RunnerFailureRule[]>

02
L1limitationexecution

02 · Failed confinement probes fail closed instead of running raw argv

SOURCE FACT

sandbox-local documents and implements SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE when a runner is unusable; it does not return the original argv.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Without bwrap or sandbox-exec, the Agent refuses to execute rather than silently falling back to the host shell.

IMPLICATION

Deployment checks should treat runner availability as a preflight gate.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 1–8
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 9–18
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

    1  /**
    2   * Local sandbox backend. It selects the platform runner chain (Linux bwrap then
    3   * Landlock; macOS Seatbelt; Windows the ACL restricted-token runner), functionally probes
    4   * competing candidates once, and reports each wrap's enforcement and stderr
    5   * classification facts. Missing or unusable confinement fails closed rather
    6   * than returning the original argv.
    7   *
    8   * The windows-acl rung additionally owns the write grants: the write SID is
    9   * the per-WORKSPACE identity derived from the canonical workspace path
   10   * (`workspaceWriteSid`), while every live session receives a RANDOM private
   11   * temp directory and its own derived capability (`tempWriteSid`). The
   12   * workspace-root ACE materializes once per workspace per server lifetime
   13   * and STANDS (the cross-session reuse cache — the exact-ACE skip makes
   14   * every later provision O(1) instead of re-propagating the tree per
   15   * session); the private-temp ACEs are revoked on dispose. The runner
   16   * receives both SIDs (their presence marks the seam-managed contract) and
   17   * stops managing DACLs itself. The rung reports partial enforcement because
   18   * WRITE_RESTRICTED must retain Everyone in its
   19   * restricting list and NTFS hard links alias one file object across paths.
   20   * @module @deepseek-ai/dsh-sandbox-local
   21   */
   22

   43  /** Plugin config. All optional — `static Config` supplies the defaults. */
   44  export interface Config {
   45    /**
   46     * Override the runner argv; bwrap-compatible profile arguments are appended. A
   47     * non-empty override asserts full enforcement and skips built-in selection and
   48     * probing. A runner that starts but refuses its profile must be identifiable by
   49     * {@link runnerFailureSignatures}. Consumers classify a spawn rejection only after
   50     * confirming the workdir is usable. `ENOENT` or `EACCES` identifies the runner when
   51     * `error.path` equals argv[0] and `error.syscall` is `spawn` or `spawn <runner>`, or
   52     * when `error.path` is absent and `error.syscall` is exactly `spawn <runner>`.
   53     */
   54    runnerCommand?: string[]
   55    /**
   56     * Case-insensitive stderr substrings emitted when a configured
   57     * {@link runnerCommand} refuses its profile before executing the wrapped
   58     * command. Required and non-empty with `runnerCommand`; rejected without
   59     * it. Each entry is a non-empty, single-line, case-insensitive substring
   60     * covering the executable runner's own failure dialect.
   61     */

   63    /** Positive timeout for each functional probe; zero would mean unbounded to Node. */
   64    probeTimeoutMs?: number
   65  }

   67  /** Probe whether `bwrap` can create the profile; the provider caches the bounded result. */
   68  function defaultProbeBwrap(timeoutMs: number): boolean {
   69    const probe = spawnSync('bwrap', ['--ro-bind', '/', '/', '--dev', '/dev', '--proc', '/proc', '--die-with-parent', '--', 'true'], {
   70      timeout: timeoutMs,
   71      stdio: 'ignore',
   72    })
   73    return probe.status === 0
   74  }
   75
   76  /**
   77   * Functional Seatbelt probe: apply the real `read-only` profile through
   78   * `sandbox-exec -p` and run `true` under it — exit 0 means the kernel
   79   * accepted and enforced the profile (`sandbox-exec` exits non-zero when
   80   * `sandbox_init` refuses it). A missing `sandbox-exec` (every non-macOS
   81   * host) fails the spawn and probes `unusable`, exactly like the other
   82   * rungs' absent binaries. Apple marks the CLI deprecated but ships it on
   83   * every macOS; if it ever disappears, this probe is what fails closed.
   84   */
   85  function defaultProbeSeatbelt(seatbeltExec: string, timeoutMs: number): boolean {
   86    const probe = spawnSync(seatbeltExec, [...seatbeltProfileArgs({ mode: 'read-only', workspaceRoot: '/' }), '--', 'true'], {
   87      timeout: timeoutMs,
   88      stdio: 'ignore',
   89    })

05
DIMENSION · TOOLS

Tool registration & execution

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1facttools

01 · Tool calls are grouped by executionMode before dispatch

SOURCE FACT

executeToolCalls parses every tool call, puts consecutive parallel calls in a bounded pool, and treats exclusive calls as barriers.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Safe parallel reads can run together while conflict-prone writes form a queue.

IMPLICATION

Concurrency policy should be part of the tool declaration, not an ad hoc model decision.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 59–78
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 79–100
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/core/agent-loop/src/tool-calls.ts:59–100PINNED ↗
   59  export async function executeToolCalls(
   60    ctx: Context,
   61    turn: number,
   62    step: number,
   63    toolCalls: ToolCallBlock[],
   64    signal: AbortSignal,
   65    acceptContext: (context: UserMessage) => void,
   66  ): Promise<{ concluded: boolean }> {
   67    const agent = ctx.agents.requireInitiator()
   68    const { session } = agent
   69
   70    // Inputs are distinct because tools/execute wrappers may replace `exec.signal`.
   71    const planned: PlannedCall[] = toolCalls.map(block => ({
   72      block,
   73      exec: {
   74        callId: block.id,
   75        name: block.name,
   76        arguments: parseArguments(block.arguments),
   77        agent,
   78        signal,
   79      },
   80    }))
   81
   82    let next = 0
   83    let concluded = false
   84    while (next < planned.length) {
   85      // Commit before classifying again so registry changes affect unstarted calls.
   86      // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded by the loop condition
   87      const first = planned[next]!
   88      const mode = ctx.tools.executionMode(first.exec).kind
   89      const group = mode === 'parallel' ? planned.slice(next) : [first]
   90      const outcome = await runGroup(
   91        ctx, turn, step, group, mode, signal, acceptContext,
   92      )
   93      next += outcome.consumed
   94      concluded ||= outcome.concluded
   95      if (outcome.aborted) {
   96        for (const call of planned.slice(next)) appendSkippedToolCall(session, turn, step, call.block)
   97        return { concluded }
   98      }
   99    }
  100    return { concluded }
  101  }
  102
  103  /** Parse model arguments, preserving invalid JSON as text and mapping empty input to `{}`. */
  104  function parseArguments(raw: string): unknown {
  105    try {
  106      return raw ? JSON.parse(raw) : {}
  107    } catch {
  108      return raw
  109    }
  110  }
  111
  112  /**
  113   * Run one exclusive barrier or parallel pool. Later calls are reclassified
  114   * before start; an exclusive reclassification waits for the current pool to
  115   * drain and remains for the caller's next barrier. Results and contexts commit
  116   * in model order. Abort stops starts, drains and commits started calls, accepts
  117   * their contexts into the owning batch, records results for skipped calls, and
  118   * returns an aborted outcome. Scheduler failure drains dispatches without
  119   * committing synthetic recovery results.
  120   */
  121  async function runGroup(
  122    ctx: Context,
  123    turn: number,
  124    step: number,
  125    group: PlannedCall[],
  126    mode: ToolExecutionMode['kind'],
  127    signal: AbortSignal,
  128    acceptContext: (context: UserMessage) => void,
  129  ): Promise<GroupOutcome> {
  130    const { session } = ctx.agents.requireInitiator()
  131    const { maxParallelToolCalls } = ctx.agentLoop.config
  132    const slots: (Slot | undefined)[] = group.map(() => undefined)
  133    // Started slots retain their `tool/call` seq so the result can cite it.
  134    const callSeqs: number[] = group.map(() => -1)
  135    let nextToStart = 0
  136    let committed = 0
  137    let started = 0
  138    let aborted: boolean = signal.aborted
  139    let concluded = false
  140    let schedulerFailure: { error: unknown } | undefined
  141    const throwSchedulerFailure = (): void => {
  142      if (schedulerFailure !== undefined) throw schedulerFailure.error
  143    }
  144
  145    // `committed` advances only across contiguous model-order slots.
  146    const commitReady = async (): Promise<void> => {
  147      while (committed < group.length) {
  148        const slot = slots[committed]
  149        if (slot === undefined) break
  150        const call = group[committed]
  151        const result = slot.needsPost
  152          ? await ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].finalize(slot.exec, slot.result)
  153          : ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].finish(slot.exec, slot.result)
  154        // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded index
  155        appendToolResult(session, turn, step, call!.block, result, callSeqs[committed]!)
  156        for (const context of result.additionalContexts ?? []) acceptContext(context)
  157        concluded ||= result.concludesTurn === true
  158        committed++
  159      }
  160    }
  161
  162    const inFlight = new Map<number, Promise<number>>()
  163
  164    const startCall = async (index: number): Promise<void> => {
  165      // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded index
  166      const call = group[index]!
  167      callSeqs[index] = appendToolCall(session, turn, step, call.block)
  168      started++
  169      const prepared = await ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].prepare(call.exec)
  170      throwSchedulerFailure()
  171      switch (prepared.kind) {

02
L1facttools

02 · Tool results may finish asynchronously but commit in model order

SOURCE FACT

runGroup stores results in slots; commitReady finalizes contiguous indexes in order, appends tool results, and queues additionalContexts for the next step.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

A third tool finishing first cannot place its receipt before the second tool in model history.

IMPLICATION

Concurrent tools need stable call ids, commit order, and synthetic results for cancellation.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 112–134
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 135–159
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/core/agent-loop/src/tool-calls.ts:112–159PINNED ↗
  112  /**
  113   * Run one exclusive barrier or parallel pool. Later calls are reclassified
  114   * before start; an exclusive reclassification waits for the current pool to
  115   * drain and remains for the caller's next barrier. Results and contexts commit
  116   * in model order. Abort stops starts, drains and commits started calls, accepts
  117   * their contexts into the owning batch, records results for skipped calls, and
  118   * returns an aborted outcome. Scheduler failure drains dispatches without
  119   * committing synthetic recovery results.
  120   */
  121  async function runGroup(
  122    ctx: Context,
  123    turn: number,
  124    step: number,
  125    group: PlannedCall[],
  126    mode: ToolExecutionMode['kind'],
  127    signal: AbortSignal,
  128    acceptContext: (context: UserMessage) => void,
  129  ): Promise<GroupOutcome> {
  130    const { session } = ctx.agents.requireInitiator()
  131    const { maxParallelToolCalls } = ctx.agentLoop.config
  132    const slots: (Slot | undefined)[] = group.map(() => undefined)
  133    // Started slots retain their `tool/call` seq so the result can cite it.
  134    const callSeqs: number[] = group.map(() => -1)
  135    let nextToStart = 0
  136    let committed = 0
  137    let started = 0
  138    let aborted: boolean = signal.aborted
  139    let concluded = false
  140    let schedulerFailure: { error: unknown } | undefined
  141    const throwSchedulerFailure = (): void => {
  142      if (schedulerFailure !== undefined) throw schedulerFailure.error
  143    }
  144
  145    // `committed` advances only across contiguous model-order slots.
  146    const commitReady = async (): Promise<void> => {
  147      while (committed < group.length) {
  148        const slot = slots[committed]
  149        if (slot === undefined) break
  150        const call = group[committed]
  151        const result = slot.needsPost
  152          ? await ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].finalize(slot.exec, slot.result)
  153          : ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].finish(slot.exec, slot.result)
  154        // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded index
  155        appendToolResult(session, turn, step, call!.block, result, callSeqs[committed]!)
  156        for (const context of result.additionalContexts ?? []) acceptContext(context)
  157        concluded ||= result.concludesTurn === true
  158        committed++
  159      }
  160    }
  161
  162    const inFlight = new Map<number, Promise<number>>()
  163
  164    const startCall = async (index: number): Promise<void> => {
  165      // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded index
  166      const call = group[index]!
  167      callSeqs[index] = appendToolCall(session, turn, step, call.block)
  168      started++
  169      const prepared = await ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].prepare(call.exec)
  170      throwSchedulerFailure()
  171      switch (prepared.kind) {
  172        case 'dispatch': {
  173          const promise = ctx.tools[TOOL_RUNTIME_SCHEDULER].dispatch(prepared.exec).then(
  174            (outcome) => {
  175              slots[index] = { exec: prepared.exec, result: outcome.result, needsPost: outcome.kind === 'post-result' }
  176              return index
  177            },
  178            (error: unknown) => {
  179              schedulerFailure ??= { error }
  180              return index
  181            },
  182          )
  183          inFlight.set(index, promise)
  184          break
  185        }
  186        case 'post-result':
  187          slots[index] = { exec: prepared.exec, result: prepared.result, needsPost: true }
  188          break
  189        case 'final-result':
  190          slots[index] = { exec: prepared.exec, result: prepared.result, needsPost: false }
  191          break
  192        /* v8 ignore next -- closed-union exhaustiveness guard */
  193        default:
  194          assertNever(prepared, 'tool-call scheduler prepare result')
  195      }
  196    }
  197
  198    const fillPool = async (): Promise<void> => {
  199      while (!aborted && nextToStart < group.length && inFlight.size < maxParallelToolCalls) {
  200        // Re-read later modes after ordered commits so registry changes can create a barrier.
  201        // oxlint-disable-next-line typescript/no-non-null-assertion -- bounded by the loop condition
  202        const nextCall = group[nextToStart]!
  203        if (nextToStart > 0 && mode === 'parallel'
  204          && ctx.tools.executionMode(nextCall.exec).kind !== 'parallel') break
  205        await startCall(nextToStart)
  206        nextToStart++
  207        throwSchedulerFailure()
  208        await commitReady()
  209        throwSchedulerFailure()
  210        // Abort may arrive while pre-execute awaits.
  211        if (signal.aborted) aborted = true
  212      }
  213    }
  214
  215    // Ordered pre-execute may await; only dispatch/body overlaps. A scheduler
  216    // failure stops new dispatches and reaches the turn boundary after every
  217    // already-started dispatch settles.
  218    try {
  219      await fillPool()
  220      while (inFlight.size > 0) {
  221        const settledIndex = await Promise.race(inFlight.values())
  222        inFlight.delete(settledIndex)
  223        throwSchedulerFailure()
  224        await commitReady()
  225        throwSchedulerFailure()
  226        // Abort may arrive while a tool or ordered commit awaits.
  227
  228        if (signal.aborted) aborted = true
  229        await fillPool()
  230      }

06
DIMENSION · CONNECTORS

Connectors, plugins & MCP

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L2factconnectors

01 · LLM, MCP, filesystem, shell, and sub-agents share capability seams

SOURCE FACT

architecture.md defines Service Definition, Provider, and Consumer as the three roles of a seam; a remote sandbox can move Bash, PTY, and LSP together.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Swapping a provider does not require duplicating logic for every tool.

IMPLICATION

Design the execution world first, then add concrete connectors.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 99–119
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 120–142
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

docs/architecture.md:99–142PINNED ↗
   99
  100  A **seam** is a swappable capability with three roles: a **Service Definition** declaring the interface, a **Service Provider** implementing it, and a **Consumer** using it, commonly a model-facing tool. A package may combine roles, but one role alone is not a seam; adding a capability means designing all three ([capability graph](capability-seams.md)).
  101
  102  Seams are why one provider swap changes the whole product. Filesystem and subprocess providers share one execution world, so pointing them at a remote sandbox moves Bash, PTY, and LSP with them, with no provider forks. [Subagent providers](subsystems/subagent.md) vary just as widely behind one interface, from a fresh child agent to a delegated turn in another product.
  103
  104  ## Where new behavior goes
  105
  106  New behavior attaches to a documented extension point. Changing the loop itself updates this map.
  107
  108  | Goal | Mechanism |
  109  |---|---|
  110  | Add a model provider | register its adapter on `ctx.llm` |
  111  | Add a model-facing capability | register on `ctx.tools`; its schema joins prompt assembly |
  112  | Give one session a different capability set | compose an agent preset; a service row there needs an `isolate` realm |
  113  | Add shell execution | register a `ctx.shell` backend; the local one spawns through `ctx.subprocess` |
  114  | Add persistent terminal execution | register a `ctx.terminals` backend plus `dsh-tool-terminal` |
  115  | Add a human command | register on `ctx.commands`; it dispatches without a model turn |
  116  | Add background work | register on `ctx.jobs`; `job_*` tools collect or stop it |
  117  | Add filesystem access or policy | register a `ctx.fs` provider or listen to `fs/*` events |
  118  | Confine spawned processes | use a `ctx.sandbox` backend; consumers wrap argv before spawning |
  119  | Intercept a request, tool, or turn | use its `agent/*` or `tools/*` event; `agent/turn-stopping` stops a turn |
  120  | Add model-facing context | call `agent.inject()`; it lands in the next admitted request |
  121  | Add UI or editor integration | drive `ctx.agents` and render from `session/event` |
  122  | Add a Web Client Chat node | register a `ConversationNodeDefinition` + keyed renderer |
  123  | Add durable session state | extend `SessionEventMap`; render and replay from the log |
  124  | Generate session titles | register the sole `ctx.sessionTitle` provider |
  125  | Manage a same-session objective | use `ctx.goals`; continue through `agent/*` |
  126  | Fork a live session | `ctx.sessions.fork(source, boundary?, childSessionId?)` |
  127  | Scope a registration to one agent | use that agent's `agent.ctx` |
  128
  129  The [extension cookbook](cookbook/extension-cookbook.md) maps features to capabilities and indexes the step-by-step guides for [packages](cookbook/adding-a-package.md), [tools](cookbook/adding-a-tool.md), [LLM adapters](cookbook/adding-an-llm-adapter.md), and [Chat nodes](cookbook/adding-a-conversation-node.md).
  130

02
L1factconnectors

02 · The tool pipeline separates decisions from observation with waterfall and emit

SOURCE FACT

tools/pre-execute can allow/deny/ask, tools/execute wraps dispatch, tools/post-execute may transform results, and tools/result observes the frozen final outcome.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Approval, retry, result transformation, and metrics are pluggable stages rather than hidden branches in one function.

IMPLICATION

Document whether each stage can block, persist, or transform data.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 137–171
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 172–207
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/core/tools/src/index.ts:137–207PINNED ↗
  137  declare module '@deepseek-ai/cordis' {
  138    interface Context {
  139      tools: ToolRuntime
  140    }
  141
  142    interface Events {
  143      /**
  144       * Allow, deny, or ask before dispatch. `next()` delegates to allow; missing
  145       * approval support turns `ask` into denial. Async gates must observe
  146       * `exec.signal`; the registry rechecks cancellation after they settle but
  147       * never abandons their promise.
  148       * Scope-filtered dispatch (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-scope`): agent-scoped listeners receive only that agent's calls.
  149       * @param exec - the pending call (name, parsed arguments, caller agent).
  150       * @mode waterfall
  151       */
  152      'tools/pre-execute'(this: Scoped<ToolRuntime>, exec: ToolExecution, next: () => Promise<PreToolDecision>): Promise<PreToolDecision>
  153      /**
  154       * Around-dispatch waterfall for timeout, retry, or metrics. `next()` returns
  155       * a normalized result; wrappers may change only `exec.signal`, while call
  156       * identity remains immutable. The registry re-fuses the original caller
  157       * signal before the body, so replacement cannot detach caller cancellation;
  158       * wrappers must still restore their signal and reach quiescence.
  159       * Scope-filtered dispatch (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-scope`): agent-scoped listeners receive only that agent's calls.
  160       * @param exec - the allowed call about to dispatch (name, parsed arguments, caller agent, signal).
  161       * @mode waterfall
  162       */
  163      'tools/execute'(this: Scoped<ToolRuntime>, exec: ToolDispatchExecution, next: () => Promise<ToolExecutionResult>): Promise<ToolExecutionResult>
  164      /**
  165       * Accept, replace, enrich, or block a normalized dispatch result. `next()`
  166       * accepts it unchanged; thrown tools still reach this waterfall as errors. Async
  167       * listeners must observe `exec.signal`; after they settle, caller
  168       * cancellation replaces only a successful accepted outcome with the code
  169       * selected by whether the tool body was invoked.
  170       * Scope-filtered dispatch (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-scope`): agent-scoped listeners receive only that agent's calls.
  171       * @param exec - the call that just ran (name, parsed arguments, caller agent).
  172       * @param result - the dispatch outcome a listener may accept, replace, or block.
  173       * @mode waterfall
  174       */
  175      'tools/post-execute'(this: Scoped<ToolRuntime>, exec: ToolExecution, result: Readonly<ToolExecutionResult>, next: () => Promise<PostToolDecision>): Promise<PostToolDecision>
  176      /**
  177       * Allow a listener to replace content in the DURABLE LOG COPY of one
  178       * `run_code` sub-dispatch outcome before the bridge appends its
  179       * `tool/code-dispatch` event. `next()` keeps the
  180       * content unchanged; a listener may return replacement blocks (e.g. the
  181       * spill policy's preview + locator for an oversized text result). Only the
  182       * logged copy is affected — the program already received the complete
  183       * value, and the model sees neither. A throwing listener is contained:
  184       * the bridge falls back to logging the original settled content.
  185       * Scope-filtered dispatch (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-scope`): agent-scoped listeners receive only that agent's dispatches.
  186       * @param dispatch - the parent execution, sub-call identity, and the settled content to log.
  187       * @mode waterfall
  188       */
  189      'tools/code-dispatch-log'(this: Scoped<ToolRuntime>, dispatch: CodeDispatchLog, next: () => Promise<ContentBlock[]>): Promise<ContentBlock[]>
  190      /**
  191       * Observe the frozen, lossless-JSON final outcome. Listener failures are contained.
  192       * Scope-filtered dispatch (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-scope`): keyed by `exec.agent`.
  193       * @param exec - the execution object that traversed the pipeline.
  194       * @param result - a deep-frozen snapshot of the final returned result.
  195       * @mode emit
  196       */
  197      'tools/result'(this: Scoped<ToolRuntime>, exec: Readonly<ToolExecution>, result: Readonly<ToolExecutionResult>): undefined
  198      /**
  199       * A tool was registered or unregistered, or a scoped restriction changed
  200       * (the available tool set changed — possibly for one scope only). An
  201       * UNFILTERED registry-subject notification, deliberately not scope-filtered
  202       * dispatch: a global change concerns every agent's next assembly, so a
  203       * scoped listener subscribing here sees every change, not just its own
  204       * scope's.
  205       * @mode emit
  206       */
  207      'tools/change'(): void
  208    }
  209  }
  210
  211  /** Tool-owned canonical output contract used after the body returns a JSON value. */
  212  export interface ToolOutputDefinition {
  213    /** Raw supported JSON Schema enforced against every successful canonical value. */
  214    readonly schema: JsonSchemaNode
  215    /** Pure projection from validated arguments and value to Native/model content. */
  216    render(args: unknown, value: JsonValue): ContentBlock[]
  217    /** Pure replayable presentation projection, computed only for top-level calls. */
  218    presentationMeta?(args: unknown, value: JsonValue): JsonValue
  219  }
  220
  221  /** A registered tool: its schema plus the execution function. */
  222  export interface ToolDefinition extends ToolSchema {
  223    /** Mandatory canonical output declaration. */
  224    readonly output: ToolOutputDefinition
  225    /**
  226     * Run one accepted call and return only its canonical lossless-JSON value.
  227     * Async work must observe or forward `exec.signal` and settle only after its
  228     * owned work reaches quiescence. The registry preserves caller cancellation
  229     * through around-dispatch signal replacement and does not abandon this
  230     * promise, but it cannot hard-kill same-process code.
  231     * @param args - losslessly snapshotted, frozen model arguments.
  232     * @param exec - execution identity, cancellation signal, and context deferral.
  233     * @returns the canonical value declared by `output.schema`.
  234     */
  235    execute(args: unknown, exec: ToolRunContext): Promise<unknown>
  236    /**
  237     * Synchronous last-mile transform for model-facing content. The registry
  238     * snapshots this callback when execution starts and invokes it exactly once
  239     * for every normalized outcome, including pipeline failures that bypass
  240     * `tools/post-execute`, immediately before lossless materialization.
  241     * Returning `undefined` preserves the content; every other result field
  242     * remains registry-owned. The callback must be total and must not throw.
  243     * @param exec - immutable execution identity and arguments.
  244     * @param result - complete normalized outcome before materialization.
  245     * @returns replacement content, or `undefined` to preserve it.
  246     */
  247    finalizeContent?(exec: Readonly<ToolExecution>, result: Readonly<ToolExecutionResult>): ContentBlock[] | undefined
  248    /**
  249     * Cooperative tool-call timeout budget in milliseconds. Omit for no deadline.
  250     * Enforced by `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-call-timeout-policy` (a `tools/execute` wrapper); it
  251     * is NEVER sent to the model — `schemas()` whitelists only name/description/
  252     * parameters. Declaring it asserts this tool forwards `exec.signal` to a
  253     * cooperative implementation that can reach quiescence when the signal aborts.
  254     */
  255    timeoutMs?: number
  256    /**
  257     * Pure synchronous classifier for overlap with sibling tool calls. Only
  258     * `true` opts in; omission, exceptions, non-`true` returns, and invalid
  259     * `defineTool` arguments are exclusive. This metadata is never model-visible.
  260     *
  261     * Opted-in executions must not mutate parent-owned state. Shared state must
  262     * tolerate concurrent dispatch; recorder races are permitted only when they
  263     * commute or fail closed. See the
  264     * [parallel-tool-call Agent Note](../../../../.agents/notes/implemented/feature/2026-07-10-parallel-tool-call-execution.md)
  265     * for the full contract.
  266     * @param args - parsed arguments; `defineTool` validates before calling.
  267     * @returns Whether this call may join a parallel group.
  268     */
  269    isConcurrencySafe?(args: unknown): boolean
  270    /**
  271     * Optional: how to present the PENDING state of one call in a UI, derived from
  272     * the call's `args` (parsed arguments, `unknown` — the tool validates/narrows
  273     * its own input). Returns a {@link ToolCallView} (a `card`-tagged render intent),
  274     * or `undefined` (or omit the method) to fall back to a generic presentation
  275     * (title = tool name, raw args as input). Pure and side-effect-free: a UI may
  276     * call it during live streaming AND a session-log replay, so it must depend
  277     * only on `args`.
  278     */

01
L2factsecurity

01 · Approval and sandboxing are separate control axes

SOURCE FACT

Tool ask/deny/allow is a policy decision, while sandbox-local confine wraps argv in an OS runner; they live in separate packages and seams.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Asking the user for permission does not replace limiting which files a command can touch.

IMPLICATION

Security UI and audit logs should show approval and enforcement separately.

DIMENSION · OBSERVABILITY

Observability & replay

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factobservability

01 · JSONL persistence avoids torn reads with a stable stat/read loop

SOURCE FACT

readStableFile compares fileRevision before and after readFile and retries if a writer appended; readRaw preserves the original JSONL text and compressed frames.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Recovery does not mistake a session being written for a complete fact.

IMPLICATION

Persistence readers must account for concurrent writers rather than calling readFile once.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 239–270
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 271–303
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/session/session-persistence-jsonl/src/index.ts:239–303PINNED ↗
  239    /**
  240     * Read a session's stored artifact text verbatim: the durable file bytes
  241     * decoded from this backend's physical encoding (complete zstd frames
  242     * concatenated, or UTF-8 plaintext). The content is the exact JSONL text the
  243     * backend wrote — never a reconstruction from parsed events — so packed-
  244     * chunk rows, key order, and line breaks survive byte-for-byte. A torn
  245     * final frame is omitted, matching the committed-prefix semantics of every
  246     * other read.
  247     * @param id - the persisted session to read.
  248     * @param signal - optional cancellation for the stat/read/decode work.
  249     * @returns the raw artifact text plus the header parsed from its own first
  250     * line, or `undefined` when the session has no stored artifact.
  251     */
  252    override async readRaw(id: SessionId, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<SessionRawArtifact | undefined> {
  253      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  254      await this.ensureRootEncoding()
  255      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  256      const path = await this.findLog(id, signal)
  257      if (path === undefined) return undefined
  258      const { buffer } = await this.readStableFile(path, signal)
  259      let content: string
  260      if (this.compression === 'zstd') {
  261        const { frames } = scanZstdFrames(buffer)
  262        if (frames.length === 0) throw new Error('empty or header-less Zstandard session log')
  263        const decoder = createZstdFrameDecoder()
  264        const plaintexts: Buffer[] = []
  265        // The decoder yields views into a reused buffer; copy each frame's
  266        // plaintext immediately so a later concat cannot read overwritten memory.
  267        for (const plaintext of decoder.decode(buffer, frames)) {
  268          signal?.throwIfAborted()
  269          plaintexts.push(Buffer.from(plaintext))
  270        }
  271        content = Buffer.concat(plaintexts).toString('utf8')
  272      } else {
  273        content = buffer.toString('utf8')
  274      }
  275      const meta = parseHeaderMeta(content.split('\n', 1)[0] as string)
  276      if (meta === undefined || meta.id !== id) {
  277        throw new Error(`corrupt session log: invalid header line in "${path}"`)
  278      }
  279      // The logical artifact name is `session.jsonl` regardless of the physical
  280      // encoding suffix (`.jsonl.zstd` marks compression only).
  281      return { meta, filename: 'session.jsonl', content }
  282    }
  283
  284    /**
  285     * Read a file's bytes under a revision-stable loop: a writer appending
  286     * between stat and readFile would yield a torn physical file, so retry
  287     * while the stat revision changes.
  288     * @param path - the artifact file to read.
  289     * @param signal - optional cancellation for the stat/read work.
  290     * @returns the stable bytes and the revision that matched both stats.
  291     */
  292    private async readStableFile(
  293      path: string,
  294      signal?: AbortSignal,
  295    ): Promise<{ buffer: Buffer; revision: PersistenceRevision }> {
  296      for (;;) {
  297        signal?.throwIfAborted()
  298        const before = fileRevision(await stat(path, { bigint: true }))
  299        const buffer = await readFile(path, { signal })
  300        signal?.throwIfAborted()
  301        const after = fileRevision(await stat(path, { bigint: true }))
  302        if (before === after) return { buffer, revision: after }
  303      }
  304    }
  305
  306    /**
  307     * Read a stored prefix and convert torn-tail state to the opaque marker the
  308     * coordinator can round-trip without knowing the physical encoding.
  309     */
  310    private async readPrefix(
  311      path: string,
  312      expectedId?: SessionId,
  313      signal?: AbortSignal,
  314    ): Promise<StoredPrefix<JsonlTornMarker>> {
  315      const { buffer, revision } = await this.readStableFile(path, signal)
  316      let prefix: Omit<StoredPrefix<JsonlTornMarker>, 'revision'>
  317      try {
  318        if (this.compression === 'zstd') {
  319          prefix = await this.readZstdPrefix(buffer, signal)
  320        } else {
  321          signal?.throwIfAborted()
  322          const { meta, events, committedBytes } = scanLog(buffer)
  323          signal?.throwIfAborted()
  324          prefix = {
  325            meta,
  326            events,
  327            ...committedBytes < buffer.byteLength
  328              ? { tornMarker: { truncateTo: committedBytes, recoveredEvents: [] } }
  329              : {},
  330          }
  331        }
  332      } catch (error: unknown) {
  333        // A parse-time format refusal predates any SessionHeader, so the
  334        // coordinator's locate-based enrichment cannot run; attach the artifact
  335        // this read actually refused.
  336        if (error instanceof SessionFormatUnsupportedError && error.location === undefined) {
  337          throw new SessionFormatUnsupportedError(`${error.message} (raw log: ${path})`, { kind: 'jsonl', path })
  338        }
  339        throw error
  340      }
  341      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  342      await this.assertStoredIdentity(path, prefix.meta, expectedId, signal)
  343      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  344      return { ...prefix, revision }
  345    }
  346
  347    /** Decode complete frames and retain complete JSONL records from a torn final frame. */
  348    private async readZstdPrefix(
  349      buffer: Buffer,
  350      signal?: AbortSignal,
  351    ): Promise<Omit<StoredPrefix<JsonlTornMarker>, 'revision'>> {
  352      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  353      const { frames, tornStart } = scanZstdFrames(buffer)
  354      signal?.throwIfAborted()
  355      if (frames.length === 0) throw new Error('empty or header-less Zstandard session log')
  356
  357      const decoder = createZstdFrameDecoder()
  358      let yieldDeadline = performance.now() + ZSTD_DECODE_YIELD_INTERVAL_MS
  359      try {
  360        const decodedFrames = decoder.decode(buffer, frames)
  361        signal?.throwIfAborted()
  362        const headerFrame = decodedFrames.next()
  363        signal?.throwIfAborted()
  364        /* v8 ignore next -- a non-empty structural frame list makes the decoder yield its first frame or throw. */
  365        if (headerFrame.done) throw new Error('empty or header-less Zstandard session log')
  366        assertZstdHeaderFrame(headerFrame.value)

  373          remainingFrames -= 1

02
L4factobservability

02 · Durable and live events are separate surfaces for replay and control

SOURCE FACT

architecture.md defines session events as durable facts, agent/* as live coordination, and capability events as policy/adapter extension points.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Replay is the black box; live control is the radio. One cannot replace the other.

IMPLICATION

Observability must state which data is recoverable and which exists only in the live process.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 67–80
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 81–95
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

docs/architecture.md:67–95PINNED ↗
   67  ```text
   68  turn/start
   69    claim next-step input plus one queued message
   70    assemble prompt sections + tool schemas
   71    -> agent/pre-step                   reject | enter(messages)
   72       reject, or a first enter rewritten empty -> close the turn with no step
   73       step/start
   74       append entered messages as user/message
   75       derive model history from the log
   76       agent/request -> llm/stream -> assistant/chunk* -> assistant/message
   77       tool/call* -> tools/pre-execute -> tools/execute -> tools/post-execute -> tool/result*
   78       step/end
   79       tools owe another request, or next-step input arrived -> claim -> next step
   80    -> agent/turn-stopping
   81  turn/end
   82  ```
   83
   84  `turn/*`, `step/*`, `user/message`, `assistant/*`, and `tool/*` are durable session events; the rest are live extension points across three domains. `agent/pre-step`, `agent/request`, `llm/stream`, and the three `tools/*` events are waterfalls, whose listeners must call `next()` to delegate; `agent/turn-stopping` is serial and has no `next()`.
   85
   86  Input reaches the driver through one inbox. Some messages wake it immediately; injected context waits in the inbox until another message does.
   87
   88  `agent/pre-step` decides what the model sees. Listeners may rewrite the claimed messages or reject them outright; a rejected or empty first claim still closes a durable turn that spent no step, so the log records the attempt. Each step reads the prompt sections and tool schemas that plugins registered.
   89
   90  Details: the [sequence diagram](agent-lifecycle.md), the [tool pipeline](tool-execution-pipeline.md), and [cancellation and error recovery](subsystems/core.md#the-agent-handle).
   91
   92  ## Session log
   93
   94  The session log is the source of the context the model sees. `deriveMessages()` projects model history from it, and raw `assistant/chunk` events preserve replay and UI fidelity. Fork, resume, transcripts, telemetry, and persistence all derive from this stream.
   95
   96  **Model-visible means logged.** Anything that reaches a model request must be reconstructable from the log, and a runtime invariant asserts it. This is why a new model-visible input requires a new session event: extend `SessionEventMap` and render from the log.
   97
   98  ## Capability seams
   99
  100  A **seam** is a swappable capability with three roles: a **Service Definition** declaring the interface, a **Service Provider** implementing it, and a **Consumer** using it, commonly a model-facing tool. A package may combine roles, but one role alone is not a seam; adding a capability means designing all three ([capability graph](capability-seams.md)).
  101
  102  Seams are why one provider swap changes the whole product. Filesystem and subprocess providers share one execution world, so pointing them at a remote sandbox moves Bash, PTY, and LSP with them, with no provider forks. [Subagent providers](subsystems/subagent.md) vary just as widely behind one interface, from a fresh child agent to a delegated turn in another product.
  103
  104  ## Where new behavior goes
  105
  106  New behavior attaches to a documented extension point. Changing the loop itself updates this map.
  107
  108  | Goal | Mechanism |
  109  |---|---|
  110  | Add a model provider | register its adapter on `ctx.llm` |
  111  | Add a model-facing capability | register on `ctx.tools`; its schema joins prompt assembly |
  112  | Give one session a different capability set | compose an agent preset; a service row there needs an `isolate` realm |
  113  | Add shell execution | register a `ctx.shell` backend; the local one spawns through `ctx.subprocess` |
  114  | Add persistent terminal execution | register a `ctx.terminals` backend plus `dsh-tool-terminal` |
  115  | Add a human command | register on `ctx.commands`; it dispatches without a model turn |
  116  | Add background work | register on `ctx.jobs`; `job_*` tools collect or stop it |
  117  | Add filesystem access or policy | register a `ctx.fs` provider or listen to `fs/*` events |
  118  | Confine spawned processes | use a `ctx.sandbox` backend; consumers wrap argv before spawning |
  119  | Intercept a request, tool, or turn | use its `agent/*` or `tools/*` event; `agent/turn-stopping` stops a turn |
  120  | Add model-facing context | call `agent.inject()`; it lands in the next admitted request |
  121  | Add UI or editor integration | drive `ctx.agents` and render from `session/event` |
  122  | Add a Web Client Chat node | register a `ConversationNodeDefinition` + keyed renderer |
  123  | Add durable session state | extend `SessionEventMap`; render and replay from the log |
  124  | Generate session titles | register the sole `ctx.sessionTitle` provider |
  125  | Manage a same-session objective | use `ctx.goals`; continue through `agent/*` |
  126  | Fork a live session | `ctx.sessions.fork(source, boundary?, childSessionId?)` |
  127  | Scope a registration to one agent | use that agent's `agent.ctx` |
  128
  129  The [extension cookbook](cookbook/extension-cookbook.md) maps features to capabilities and indexes the step-by-step guides for [packages](cookbook/adding-a-package.md), [tools](cookbook/adding-a-tool.md), [LLM adapters](cookbook/adding-an-llm-adapter.md), and [Chat nodes](cookbook/adding-a-conversation-node.md).
  130

L1factcollaboration

01 · Sub-agents have independent sessions, descriptors, and depth

SOURCE FACT

The subagent package centers child-agent, descriptors, delegation depth, lifecycle, and run settlement; child output is extracted from its own event suffix.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

A sub-agent is not a role inside the parent prompt; it owns another recoverable work ledger.

IMPLICATION

Delegation needs independent context, lifecycle, cancellation, and settlement—not another prompt.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 1–59
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 60–120
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/subagent/subagent/src/child-agent.ts:1–120PINNED ↗
    1  /**
    2   * Shared in-process child composition: the delegation-depth budget, the
    3   * durable session metadata, the resolved child `AgentOptions`, the delegated
    4   * policy seed, and the scoped setup a child agent needs. Both the one-shot
    5   * provider driver and the continuation manager compose children this way, so
    6   * depth accounting, lineage stamping, and delegation policy have one home.
    7   *
    8   * @module @deepseek-ai/dsh-subagent/child-agent
    9   */
   10
   11  import type { Context } from '@deepseek-ai/cordis'
   12  import type { Agent, AgentOptions, CreateAgentOptions } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent'
   13  import type { SandboxMode } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-sandbox'
   14  import type { Session, SessionId } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-session'
   15  import type { ToolRestriction } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-tools'
   16  // Type-only: make `ctx.get('sandboxPolicy')` / `ctx.get('approval')` resolve
   17  // to the policy services when composed — delegation consumes both
   18  // opportunistically (the documented `ctx.get` pattern), never as a hard dep —
   19  // and merge the `sandbox/mode` / `approval/policy` session-event payloads.
   20  import type {} from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-sandbox-policy'
   21  import type {} from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-user-approval'
   22  // Type-only: make `ctx.get('agentPresets')` resolve to the preset roster when
   23  // composed — a child inherits its parent's composition opportunistically (the
   24  // documented `ctx.get` pattern), never as a hard dep. A rosterless deployment
   25  // keeps its model-facing rows on the host plane, where the child already sees
   26  // them through the tool registry's global layer.
   27  import type {} from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent-presets'
   28  import { delegationDepthOf } from './depth.ts'
   29
   30  /** Thrown when starting a child would exceed the requested depth cap. */
   31  export class SubagentDepthError extends Error {
   32    constructor(public readonly attemptedDepth: number, public readonly maxDepth: number) {
   33      super(`subagent depth ${attemptedDepth} exceeds maxDepth ${maxDepth}`)
   34      this.name = 'SubagentDepthError'
   35    }
   36  }
   37
   38  /**
   39   * Resolve the child's delegation depth from its parent and enforce an optional
   40   * cap. The persisted parent header is the monotone floor, so a resumed parent
   41   * cannot delegate as if it were top-level.
   42   * @param parent - the delegating parent agent.
   43   * @param maxDepth - optional absolute cap the resolved depth must not exceed.
   44   * @returns the child's non-negative safe-integer depth.
   45   * @throws {SubagentDepthError} when the resolved depth exceeds `maxDepth`.
   46   * @throws {RangeError} when the resolved depth leaves the safe-integer range.
   47   */
   48  export function resolveChildDepth(parent: Agent, maxDepth: number | undefined): number {
   49    const childDepth = delegationDepthOf(parent) + 1
   50    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(childDepth)) {
   51      throw new RangeError('subagent child depth exceeds the safe-integer range')
   52    }
   53    if (maxDepth !== undefined && childDepth > maxDepth) {
   54      throw new SubagentDepthError(childDepth, maxDepth)
   55    }
   56    return childDepth
   57  }
   58
   59  /**
   60   * Resolve the child's `AgentOptions`: the parent's provider/model/maxTokens
   61   * route unless the request overrides it, stamped with the child's own
   62   * delegation depth.
   63   * @param parent - the delegating parent whose route the child inherits.
   64   * @param requested - per-child overrides, if any.
   65   * @param childDepth - the resolved delegation depth to stamp.
   66   * @returns the resolved options for `ctx.agents.create()`.
   67   */
   68  export function resolveChildAgentOptions(
   69    parent: Agent,
   70    requested: AgentOptions | undefined,
   71    childDepth: number,
   72  ): AgentOptions {
   73    const parentProvider = parent.options.provider
   74    const parentModel = parent.options.model
   75    const parentMaxTokens = parent.options.maxTokens
   76    return {
   77      ...parentProvider !== undefined ? { provider: parentProvider } : {},
   78      ...parentModel !== undefined ? { model: parentModel } : {},
   79      ...parentMaxTokens !== undefined ? { maxTokens: parentMaxTokens } : {},
   80      ...requested,
   81      subagentDepth: childDepth,
   82    }
   83  }
   84
   85  /**
   86   * Build the child session's durable creation metadata: the parent's workspace,
   87   * its direct lineage, coarse product origin, the recursion budget that must
   88   * survive persistence, the seed boundary that separates inherited parent
   89   * history from child work, and the composition the child runs under.
   90   *
   91   * The preset is read from the parent's LIVE scope chain rather than from its
   92   * header, because a parent that switched preset while blank runs on the newer
   93   * composition and its header still names the older one. Recording it is what
   94   * makes a child's history reconstructable: without it a cold read of the child
   95   * resolves the deployment default and rebuilds turns under a tool set the
   96   * child never had.
   97   * @param parent - the delegating parent agent.
   98   * @param childDepth - the resolved delegation depth to persist.
   99   * @param lineageSeedLength - how many leading events came from the parent's log.
  100   * @returns the `meta` for `ctx.agents.create()`.
  101   */
  102  export function childSessionMeta(
  103    parent: Agent,
  104    childDepth: number,
  105    lineageSeedLength: number,
  106  ): NonNullable<CreateAgentOptions['meta']> {
  107    const parentHeader = parent.session.header
  108    const agentPreset = parent.ctx.get('agentPresets')?.composedPreset(parent.ctx)
  109    return {
  110      ...parentHeader.cwd !== undefined ? { cwd: parentHeader.cwd } : {},
  111      ...agentPreset === undefined ? {} : { agentPreset },
  112      parentSession: parentHeader.id,
  113      // Navigation classification only; the descriptor remains the authority
  114      // for mode and continuation capability.
  115      origin: 'subagent',
  116      // Durable: the recursion budget must survive persistence and resume.
  117      delegationDepth: childDepth,
  118      ...lineageSeedLength > 0 ? { seedLength: lineageSeedLength } : {},
  119    }
  120  }
  121
  122  /** The scoped composition a child agent's creation window applies. */
  123  export interface ChildComposition {
  124    /** Per-child persona shadowing the deployment persona. */
  125    readonly persona?: string | undefined
  126    /** Per-child tool scoping. */
  127    readonly toolFilter?: ToolRestriction | undefined
  128  }
  129
  130  /**
  131   * Model-facing delegation-scope statement for every in-process child. A
  132   * runtime-context contribution rather than a system-prompt section, so the
  133   * deployment's system prompt stays uniform across parents and children.
  134   */
  135  export const SUBAGENT_DELEGATION_CONTEXT
  136    = 'You are a delegated subagent: your permission scope was fixed when you were started and cannot be '
  137      + 'widened from inside this session — operations that require approval are rejected automatically. '
  138      + 'When the task needs access beyond that scope, do not retry the denied operation; state the '
  139      + 'limitation in your reply so the delegating agent can handle it.'
  140
  141  /**
  142   * Compose one child inside its creation window: join its parent's preset,
  143   * register the fixed delegation-scope statement, then apply the child's own
  144   * shadowing persona section and tool restriction, all owned by the child's
  145   * scope and therefore invisible to its parent and siblings. Creation and cold
  146   * resume both pass through here.
  147   *
  148   * The join comes first and the child's own registrations second, which is the
  149   * order the layering already implies — the nearest scope wins a name, and a
  150   * per-child restriction intersects with everything its chain admits — but
  151   * stating it here keeps the two steps from being read as independent.
  152   *
  153   * The join and the per-child registrations live in ONE call because a child
  154   * composed without the join is exactly the defect this function exists to
  155   * prevent: with every model-facing row on the agent plane, a child that joins
  156   * no preset sees an empty tool registry and none of its parent's prompt
  157   * sections. Taking the parent as a parameter is what makes that omission
  158   * unrepresentable at the call sites.
  159   * @param childCtx - the child agent's scoped creation context.
  160   * @param parent - the delegating parent whose composition the child joins.
  161   * @param composition - the per-child persona and tool filter to install.
  162   */
  163  export function applyChildComposition(
  164    childCtx: Context,
  165    parent: Agent,
  166    composition: ChildComposition,
  167  ): void {
  168    childCtx.get('agentPresets')?.composeFrom(childCtx, parent.ctx)
  169    // Order 120: after the sandbox:policy (110) and approval:policy (115) sentences.
  170    childCtx.systemPrompt.context({ name: 'subagent:delegation', order: 120, text: SUBAGENT_DELEGATION_CONTEXT })
  171    if (composition.persona !== undefined) {
  172      childCtx.systemPrompt.section({ name: 'deployment:persona', order: 0, text: composition.persona })
  173    }
  174    if (composition.toolFilter !== undefined) childCtx.tools.restrict(composition.toolFilter)
  175  }
  176
  177  /** Policy seeded onto a child session's log at the delegation boundary. */
  178  export interface DelegatedPolicyOverrides {
  179    /** The parent session's explicit sandbox-mode override, or `undefined` without one. */
  180    readonly sandboxMode: SandboxMode | undefined
  181    /**
  182     * `'never'` whenever the approval capability is composed, `undefined`
  183     * otherwise: a delegated child acts only within the sandbox scope fixed at
  184     * delegation, so its asks are rejected deterministically.
  185     */
  186    readonly approvalPolicy: 'never' | undefined
  187  }
  188

  190   * Capture the policy to seed into one delegation. Call synchronously before
  191   * the child start's first await: a later parent switch belongs to the

02
L1factcollaboration

02 · Child-agent terminal state is derived from its own event facts

SOURCE FACT

lifecycle derives completed, error, refusal, or aborted from the child session's turn/end, dropped work, errors, and blocked events.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

A process being torn down successfully does not mean the task succeeded; the child log decides.

IMPLICATION

Collaboration results should follow task facts, not merely a thread exit code.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 220–238
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 239–258
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/subagent/subagent/src/lifecycle.ts:220–258PINNED ↗
  220   * Why this child's epoch ended, for the terminal lifecycle edge and the
  221   * manager's own parent delivery. The child's own log is authoritative:
  222   * teardown succeeding says nothing about whether the model errored, hit its
  223   * token ceiling, or was cancelled, so deriving the reason from disposal would
  224   * report failed work as completed.
  225   *
  226   * {@link foldConsumedWork} supplies both halves the raw turn sequence cannot:
  227   * which turn accounts for the work this epoch consumed, and whether accepted
  228   * work was cancelled after it without any turn opening over it. A recorded
  229   * failure still wins over a cancellation — stopping a child that had already
  230   * failed does not turn its failure into a cancellation.
  231   * @param events - this epoch's own event suffix.
  232   * @returns its terminal stop reason; `completed` only for an epoch that both
  233   *   closed cleanly and had nothing left to run.
  234   */
  235  function epochStopReason(events: readonly SessionEvent[]): SubagentResult['stopReason'] {
  236    const { end, droppedUnrun } = foldConsumedWork(events)
  237    switch (end?.data.reason.kind) {
  238      case 'max-tokens':
  239        return 'max-tokens'
  240      case 'aborted':
  241      case 'interrupted':
  242        return 'aborted'
  243      case 'error':
  244        return 'error'
  245      // A pre-step rejection — a hook deny, a policy plugin — discarded input
  246      // this epoch had claimed: the work was declined, not done.
  247      case 'blocked':
  248        return 'refusal'
  249      // A clean ending and no accounting turn at all share one rule: the epoch
  250      // finished what it was given unless a cancelled queue says otherwise.
  251      case undefined:
  252      case 'completed':
  253        return droppedUnrun ? 'aborted' : 'completed'
  254      /* v8 ignore next 3 -- `TurnEndReason` is merge-extensible, so this arm needs a
  255       * backend that adds a variant; treating an unnameable reason as success would
  256       * report failed work as completed. */
  257      default:
  258        return 'error'
  259    }
  260  }
  261
  262  /** Render any listener-thrown value without letting coercion escape containment. */
  263  function renderThrown(value: unknown): string {
  264    try {
  265      return value instanceof Error ? `${value.name}: ${value.message}` : String(value)
  266    } catch {
  267      return '<unrenderable thrown value>'
  268    }
  269  }
  270
