L2factinstructions

01 · system-prompt enforces strict sections and toolOrder rules

SOURCE FACT

system-prompt validates variables, duplicate toolOrder entries, <unlisted-tools>, and unknown tool names before rendering sections in stable order.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Prompts and tool catalogs are not arbitrary string concatenation; order and unknown entries are rejected.

IMPLICATION

Instruction assembly should fail loudly so the model's capability set cannot drift silently.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 142–161
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 162–182
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/core/system-prompt/src/index.ts:142–182PINNED ↗
  142  /**
  143   * Validate duplicate names and the required {@link TOOL_ORDER_REST} marker.
  144   * Registered names are checked later because plugins have not loaded yet.
  145   */
  146  function validateToolOrder(toolOrder: string[] | undefined): string[] | undefined {
  147    if (toolOrder === undefined) return undefined
  148    const seen = new Set<string>()
  149    for (const name of toolOrder) {
  150      if (seen.has(name)) throw new Error(`toolOrder lists "${name}" more than once`)
  151      seen.add(name)
  152    }
  153    if (!seen.has(TOOL_ORDER_REST)) {
  154      throw new Error(`toolOrder must contain the "${TOOL_ORDER_REST}" rest entry (where unlisted tools are inserted)`)
  155    }
  156    return toolOrder
  157  }
  158
  159  /**
  160   * Apply configured tool order, inserting unlisted tools lexicographically at
  161   * {@link TOOL_ORDER_REST}. Unknown configured names fail; known but restricted
  162   * names may be absent.
  163   */
  164  function orderTools(tools: ToolSchema[], toolOrder: string[] | undefined, knownNames: ReadonlySet<string>): ToolSchema[] {
  165    const reserved = tools.find(tool => tool.name === TOOL_ORDER_REST)
  166    if (reserved !== undefined) {
  167      throw new Error(`tool provider returned reserved tool name "${TOOL_ORDER_REST}" (reserved for toolOrder's rest entry)`)
  168    }
  169    if (toolOrder === undefined) return tools.sort(compareToolNames)
  170    const unknown = toolOrder.filter(name => name !== TOOL_ORDER_REST && !knownNames.has(name))
  171    if (unknown.length > 0) {
  172      throw new Error(`toolOrder lists unregistered tool${unknown.length > 1 ? 's' : ''} ${unknown.map(name => `"${name}"`).join(', ')}; known tools: ${[...knownNames].sort().join(', ') || '(none)'}`)
  173    }
  174    const listed = new Set(toolOrder)
  175    const rest = tools.filter(tool => !listed.has(tool.name)).sort(compareToolNames)
  176    return toolOrder.flatMap(name =>
  177      name === TOOL_ORDER_REST ? rest : tools.filter(tool => tool.name === name))
  178  }
  179
  180  /** Lexicographic (code-unit) name comparison — locale-independent, so the order is identical on every machine. */
  181  function compareToolNames(a: ToolSchema, b: ToolSchema): number {
  182    return a.name < b.name ? -1 : a.name > b.name ? 1 : 0
  183  }
  184
  185  /** Plugin config: the deployment-authored fragment of the system prompt (see {@link Config.persona} for its contract). */
  186  export interface Config {
  187    /** Include the fixed DeepSeek Harness identity before the deployment persona (default true). */
  188    includeHarnessIdentity?: boolean
  189    /** Include dynamic runtime-context snapshots in model history (default true). */
  190    includeRuntimeContext?: boolean
  191    /**
  192     * Deployment-wide order-0 persona template. A scoped section named
  193     * `deployment:persona` shadows it; `{{variable}}` references are strict.
  194     */
  195    persona?: string
  196    /**
  197     * Model-facing tool names in order, with {@link TOOL_ORDER_REST} exactly once.
  198     * Invalid fields fail at load and unknown names fail at assembly; known names
  199     * hidden in one scope may be absent there. Omitted means lexicographic order.
  200     */
  201    toolOrder?: string[]
  202  }
  203
  204  /**
  205   * Interpolate strict `{{variable}}` references, drop empty sections, and join
  206   * the rest with blank lines. Malformed, unknown, or undefined references throw;
  207   * a lone `{{` without any later `}}` is literal prose, and substituted values
  208   * are not scanned again.
  209   * @param assembly - the assembly whose sections and variables to render.
  210   * @returns the rendered prompt, or `''` when all sections are empty.
  211   */
  212  export function renderPrompt(assembly: PromptAssembly): string {
  213    return assembly.sections
  214      .map(section => interpolate(section, assembly.variables, 'section'))
  215      .filter(text => text.length > 0)
  216      .join('\n\n')
  217  }
  218
  219  /**
  220   * Render the complete dynamic context snapshot.
  221   * @param assembly - the assembly whose contexts and variables to render.
  222   * @returns the current full snapshot, or `''` when no context is active.
  223   */
  224  export function renderContextSnapshot(assembly: PromptAssembly): string {
  225    return joinContextSections(renderContextSections(assembly))
  226  }
  227
  228  /**
  229   * The model-facing snapshot text for an already-rendered section list.
  230   *
  231   * A caller that also needs the sections renders them once and joins here, so a
  232   * request does not interpolate every context twice.
  233   * @param sections - sections from {@link renderContextSections}.
  234   * @returns the current full snapshot, or `''` when no context is active.
  235   */
  236  export function joinContextSections(sections: readonly ContextSnapshotSection[]): string {
  237    const body = sections.map(section => section.text).join('\n\n')
  238    if (body.length === 0) return ''
  239    return `Current runtime context. This snapshot supersedes earlier runtime-context snapshots.\n\n${body}`
  240  }
  241
  242  /**
  243   * The same snapshot, kept as the named contributions it was assembled from.
  244   *
  245   * {@link renderContextSnapshot} joins these for the model; a consumer that
  246   * presents the snapshot uses them to attribute each part to the subsystem that
  247   * contributed it, without re-splitting the joined prose.
  248   * @param assembly - the assembly whose contexts and variables to render.
  249   * @returns one entry per contributing context that rendered to non-empty text.
  250   */
  251  export function renderContextSections(assembly: PromptAssembly): ContextSnapshotSection[] {
  252    return assembly.contexts
  253      .map(context => ({ name: context.name, text: interpolate(context, assembly.variables, 'context') }))

02
L4factinstructions

02 · Configuration is layered through profiles, bundles, and patches

SOURCE FACT

architecture.md defines bundle, profile, home, and --patch overlay order, and exposes dump-config to inspect the resolved runtime tree.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

The same source can power web, headless, or enterprise profiles, but the final mounted capability tree must be visible.

IMPLICATION

Treat the resolved config as a deploy-time audit artifact.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 17–31
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 32–47
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

docs/architecture.md:17–47PINNED ↗
   17  A running `dsh` is a plugin tree composed at boot from ordered layers.
   18
   19  A **profile** is a named composition stored in the Harness home. It lists the bundles it stacks, holds any out-of-tree plugins it installs, and keeps the user's own `cordis.patch.yml`. `web` and `headless` ship as templates.
   20
   21  A **bundle** is a distribution format for Cordis config rows and the code they mount, so whatever it inserts stays patchable by the layers above it.
   22
   23  Each declares itself in its own `package.json` under a `dsh` field: `dsh.profile` lists a profile's bundles, and `dsh.bundle` points at a bundle's patch file.
   24
   25  [`dsh-base`](../packages/bundle/base/README.md) is the first layer of every profile: model adapters, tools, persistence, sandbox and approval policy, settings, credentials, telemetry. [`dsh-web-app`](../packages/bundle/web-app/README.md) adds the browser application; [`dsh-headless`](../packages/bundle/headless/README.md) adds a one-shot runner with no server at all.
   26
   27  Layers apply to an empty entry list in this order: each bundle in the profile's listed order, then the profile's `cordis.patch.yml`, then the home-level one, then any `--patch` overlay. A patch targets a row by id and replaces its whole config, or inserts new rows.
   28
   29  To see the tree your machine actually boots:
   30
   31  ```sh
   32  dsh --profile web --dump-config
   33  ```
   34
   35  Any row it prints can be replaced by a patch of your own.
   36
   37  Composition mechanics are in [app-boot](../packages/boot/app-boot/README.md#profiles); config fields are in the generated [config catalog](config-catalog.md).
   38
   39  ## Core packages
   40
   41  Here are some core packages that contribute to the Cordis tree.
   42
   43  | Package | Owns | `ctx` key |
   44  |---|---|---|
   45  | [`core/session`](subsystems/session.md) | The append-only `SessionEvent` log and in-memory store | `ctx.sessions` |
   46  | [`core/system-prompt`](subsystems/system-prompt.md) | Prompt-section and tool-schema assembly | `ctx.systemPrompt` |
   47  | [`core/tools`](subsystems/tools.md) | The scoped tool registry and guarded execution pipeline | `ctx.tools` |
   48  | [`core/agent`](subsystems/core.md) | The `Agent` interface, live registry, and `agent/*` events | `ctx.agents` |
   49  | [`core/agent-loop`](subsystems/core.md) | The default driver implementing that interface | `ctx.agentLoop` |
   50  | [`core/scope`](subsystems/scope.md) | The per-agent scoped-registration primitive | library, no key |
   51  | [`llm/llm`](subsystems/llm-streaming.md) | Message and stream vocabulary plus the adapter seam | `ctx.llm` |
   52
   53  ## Events
   54
   55  Events are the extension points, and picking the right domain is the first decision in most changes.
   56
   57  - **Session events** are durable facts appended to the log and broadcast through `session/event`. Use one when the fact must survive a reload.
   58  - **Agent events** (`agent/*`) carry a live `Agent`: inbox, step, status, request, validation, continuation. Use one to observe or intercept work in flight.
   59  - **Capability events** attach policy and adapters to a seam (`fs/*`, `tools/*`, `telemetry/*`) without importing the loop.
   60
   61  The [event map](event-producer-consumer.md) lists every event's producers and consumers.
   62
   63  ## Turn flow
   64
   65  A **step** is one model request plus the tools it calls. A **turn** is zero or more steps: it opens before its first input is claimed and closes once nothing is owed.
   66
   67  ```text
   68  turn/start
   69    claim next-step input plus one queued message
   70    assemble prompt sections + tool schemas
   71    -> agent/pre-step                   reject | enter(messages)
   72       reject, or a first enter rewritten empty -> close the turn with no step
   73       step/start
   74       append entered messages as user/message
   75       derive model history from the log
   76       agent/request -> llm/stream -> assistant/chunk* -> assistant/message
   77       tool/call* -> tools/pre-execute -> tools/execute -> tools/post-execute -> tool/result*
   78       step/end
   79       tools owe another request, or next-step input arrived -> claim -> next step
   80    -> agent/turn-stopping
   81  turn/end
   82  ```
   83
   84  `turn/*`, `step/*`, `user/message`, `assistant/*`, and `tool/*` are durable session events; the rest are live extension points across three domains. `agent/pre-step`, `agent/request`, `llm/stream`, and the three `tools/*` events are waterfalls, whose listeners must call `next()` to delegate; `agent/turn-stopping` is serial and has no `next()`.
   85
   86  Input reaches the driver through one inbox. Some messages wake it immediately; injected context waits in the inbox until another message does.
   87
   88  `agent/pre-step` decides what the model sees. Listeners may rewrite the claimed messages or reject them outright; a rejected or empty first claim still closes a durable turn that spent no step, so the log records the attempt. Each step reads the prompt sections and tool schemas that plugins registered.
   89
   90  Details: the [sequence diagram](agent-lifecycle.md), the [tool pipeline](tool-execution-pipeline.md), and [cancellation and error recovery](subsystems/core.md#the-agent-handle).
   91
   92  ## Session log
   93
   94  The session log is the source of the context the model sees. `deriveMessages()` projects model history from it, and raw `assistant/chunk` events preserve replay and UI fidelity. Fork, resume, transcripts, telemetry, and persistence all derive from this stream.
   95
   96  **Model-visible means logged.** Anything that reaches a model request must be reconstructable from the log, and a runtime invariant asserts it. This is why a new model-visible input requires a new session event: extend `SessionEventMap` and render from the log.
   97
   98  ## Capability seams
   99
  100  A **seam** is a swappable capability with three roles: a **Service Definition** declaring the interface, a **Service Provider** implementing it, and a **Consumer** using it, commonly a model-facing tool. A package may combine roles, but one role alone is not a seam; adding a capability means designing all three ([capability graph](capability-seams.md)).
  101
  102  Seams are why one provider swap changes the whole product. Filesystem and subprocess providers share one execution world, so pointing them at a remote sandbox moves Bash, PTY, and LSP with them, with no provider forks. [Subagent providers](subsystems/subagent.md) vary just as widely behind one interface, from a fresh child agent to a delegated turn in another product.
  103
  104  ## Where new behavior goes
  105
  106  New behavior attaches to a documented extension point. Changing the loop itself updates this map.
  107
  108  | Goal | Mechanism |
  109  |---|---|
  110  | Add a model provider | register its adapter on `ctx.llm` |
  111  | Add a model-facing capability | register on `ctx.tools`; its schema joins prompt assembly |
  112  | Give one session a different capability set | compose an agent preset; a service row there needs an `isolate` realm |
  113  | Add shell execution | register a `ctx.shell` backend; the local one spawns through `ctx.subprocess` |
  114  | Add persistent terminal execution | register a `ctx.terminals` backend plus `dsh-tool-terminal` |
  115  | Add a human command | register on `ctx.commands`; it dispatches without a model turn |
  116  | Add background work | register on `ctx.jobs`; `job_*` tools collect or stop it |
  117  | Add filesystem access or policy | register a `ctx.fs` provider or listen to `fs/*` events |
  118  | Confine spawned processes | use a `ctx.sandbox` backend; consumers wrap argv before spawning |

DIMENSION · RECOVERY

Recovery, tests & trade-offs

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factrecovery

01 · Compaction runs at pre-step pressure and canonical overflow

SOURCE FACT

agent-lifecycle documents compaction-basic handling pressure at agent/pre-step and canonical overflow at agent/request-error; the failed step closes before a retry turn is considered.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Compaction does not suddenly delete history; it reorganizes the request after a failed step is closed.

IMPLICATION

Compaction, retry, and event closure need an explicit order to avoid orphan tool results.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 73–83
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 84–95
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

docs/agent-lifecycle.md:73–95PINNED ↗
   73
   74  The `assistant/message` event records every successful provider call, including content-less and `max-tokens` finishes. Empty content stays out of derived history, while the durable event keeps usage and `sourceEventSeqs` listing the exact `assistant/chunk` events, including an explicit empty list.
   75
   76  `dsh-compaction-basic` uses `agent/pre-step` for pressure before request derivation and `agent/request-error` only for canonical context overflow. Once either trigger qualifies, optional tool-result pruning runs before summary selection. Recovery works between the closed failed step and failed turn close, and opens a fresh retry turn only when pruning or summarization advances the surface replacement generation; otherwise the original request error remains authoritative.
   77
   78  The returned `agent/pre-step` decision is authoritative; listeners wrapping `next()` preserve downstream messages unless replacement is intentional. Steering and injected context pass through the same waterfall after a later claim operation takes their next-step batch.
   79
   80  SDK users that need replayable transcript data should consume `session/event`; `agent/*` is the live coordination API for queue/status, prompt interception, request construction, steering, continuation, and errors.
   81

02
L1factrecovery

02 · JSONL records version, parent session, delegation depth, and encoding

SOURCE FACT

HeaderLine carries version, id, cwd, parentSession, origin, delegationDepth, and agentPreset; logPath selects zstd or plaintext artifacts.

PLAIN LANGUAGE

Recovery first identifies the ledger, its parent, and delegation depth before reading later events.

IMPLICATION

A session header should be a verifiable migration protocol, not a few debug fields.

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 29–56
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 57–85
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

packages/session/session-persistence-jsonl/src/format.ts:29–85PINNED ↗
   29   * The first JSONL record of a session artifact: the immutable
   30   * {@link SessionHeader} tagged as a `session` record so a reader can tell it
   31   * apart from an event line.
   32   */
   33  export interface HeaderLine {
   34    type: 'session'
   35    version: number
   36    id: SessionId
   37    createdAt: number
   38    cwd?: string
   39    parentSession?: SessionId
   40    seedLength?: number
   41    origin?: 'subagent'
   42    delegationDepth: number
   43    agentPreset?: string
   44  }
   45
   46  /**
   47   * Build the header line object from a {@link SessionHeader}.
   48   * @param header - the immutable session metadata to serialize.
   49   * @returns the `type: 'session'`-tagged line object, absent optional fields omitted (never null).
   50   */
   51  export function toHeaderLine(header: SessionHeader): HeaderLine {
   52    return {
   53      type: 'session',
   54      version: header.version,
   55      id: header.id,
   56      createdAt: header.createdAt,
   57      ...header.cwd !== undefined ? { cwd: header.cwd } : {},
   58      ...header.parentSession !== undefined ? { parentSession: header.parentSession } : {},
   59      ...header.seedLength !== undefined ? { seedLength: header.seedLength } : {},
   60      ...header.origin !== undefined ? { origin: header.origin } : {},
   61      delegationDepth: header.delegationDepth ?? 0,
   62      ...header.agentPreset !== undefined ? { agentPreset: header.agentPreset } : {},
   63    }
   64  }
   65
   66  /**
   67   * Parse a header line back into a {@link SessionHeader}.
   68   * @param line - the shape-checked first line of a log (see the `isHeaderLine` guard).
   69   * @returns the header, absent optional fields omitted.
   70   */
   71  export function fromHeaderLine(line: HeaderLine): SessionHeader {
   72    if (Object.hasOwn(line, 'sandboxMode') || Object.hasOwn(line, 'approvalPolicy')) {
   73      throw new Error('session header uses retired policy baseline fields')
   74    }
   75    return {
   76      version: line.version,
   77      id: line.id,
   78      createdAt: line.createdAt,
   79      ...line.cwd !== undefined ? { cwd: line.cwd } : {},
   80      ...line.parentSession !== undefined ? { parentSession: line.parentSession } : {},
   81      ...line.seedLength !== undefined ? { seedLength: line.seedLength } : {},
   82      ...line.origin !== undefined ? { origin: line.origin } : {},
   83      delegationDepth: line.delegationDepth,
   84      ...line.agentPreset !== undefined ? { agentPreset: line.agentPreset } : {},
   85    }
   86  }
   87
   88  /** Type guard: a parsed first line is a well-formed session header. */
   89  function isHeaderLine(value: unknown): value is HeaderLine {
   90    return (
   91      typeof value === 'object' && value !== null
   92      && (value as { type?: unknown }).type === 'session'
   93      && typeof (value as { version?: unknown }).version === 'number'
   94      && typeof (value as { id?: unknown }).id === 'string'
   95      && typeof (value as { createdAt?: unknown }).createdAt === 'number'
   96      && Number.isSafeInteger((value as { createdAt: number }).createdAt)
   97      && (value as { createdAt: number }).createdAt >= 0
   98      && !Object.is((value as { createdAt: number }).createdAt, -0)
   99      && typeof (value as { delegationDepth?: unknown }).delegationDepth === 'number'
  100      && Number.isSafeInteger((value as { delegationDepth: number }).delegationDepth)
  101      && (value as { delegationDepth: number }).delegationDepth >= 0
  102      && !Object.is((value as { delegationDepth: number }).delegationDepth, -0)
  103      && ((value as { origin?: unknown }).origin === undefined
  104        || (value as { origin?: unknown }).origin === 'subagent')
  105      && ((value as { agentPreset?: unknown }).agentPreset === undefined
  106        || typeof (value as { agentPreset?: unknown }).agentPreset === 'string')
  107    )
  108  }
  109
  110  /**
  111   * Encode an arbitrary string as a single safe path segment, injectively over ALL JS (UTF-16)
  112   * strings — including lone surrogates. A {@link SessionId} is an unvalidated branded string,
  113   * so this neutralizes `../`, absolute paths, NUL, and separators before any filesystem use.
  114   * Safe code units remain literal; every other unit, including `~`, becomes
  115   * `~XXXX`. Operating on code units preserves lone surrogates, while special-
  116   * casing `.` and `..` prevents traversal by an otherwise safe whole segment.
  117   *
  118   * @param raw - the string to encode; must be non-empty (throws on `''`).
  119   * @returns the escaped single path segment, decodable back to `raw`.
  120   */
  121  export function encodeSegment(raw: string): string {
  122    if (raw.length === 0) throw new Error('cannot encode an empty path segment')
  123    if (raw === '.') return '~002E'
  124    if (raw === '..') return '~002E~002E'
  125    let out = ''
  126    for (let i = 0; i < raw.length; i++) {
  127      const code = raw.charCodeAt(i)
  128      const ch = String.fromCharCode(code)
  129      if (ch !== '~' && /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]$/.test(ch)) {
  130        out += ch
  131      } else {
  132        out += '~' + code.toString(16).toUpperCase().padStart(4, '0')
  133      }
  134    }
  135    return out
  136  }
  137
  138  /**
  139   * Build the readable directory key for a project path.
  140   * Filesystem separators and drive separators become `-`; unsafe code units use
  141   * the same `~XXXX` escape as session ids. The key is bounded for filesystem
  142   * component limits. Separator replacement and truncation are intentionally
  143   * lossy, following the common human-navigable project-directory convention.
  144   * @param cwd - the session's project directory.
  145   * @returns a single filesystem-safe project directory name.
  146   */
  147  export function projectKey(cwd: string): string {
  148    if (cwd.length === 0) throw new Error('cannot encode an empty project path')
  149    let readable = ''
  150    let separatorRun = false
  151    for (let i = 0; i < cwd.length; i++) {
  152      const code = cwd.charCodeAt(i)
  153      const ch = String.fromCharCode(code)
  154      if (ch === '/' || ch === '\\' || ch === ':') {
  155        if (!separatorRun) readable += '-'
  156        separatorRun = true
